# Easywurld — 30 Day Social Plan
### 11 core posts a day, restructured for TikTok, Instagram and Facebook

**Every post is ready to publish.** Hook, beats, voiceover, slide copy, caption, hashtags and
visual direction are all written. No placeholders, nothing to decide on the day.

| | |
|---|---|
| Plan starts | **Sunday 1 November 2026** (change it in the app if you want a different month) |
| Ends | **Saturday 21 November 2026** |
| Daily volume | 11 core posts → 11 TikTok + 11 Instagram + 11 Facebook = **33 posts a day** |
| Month total | **693 posts** (231 core ideas x 3 platforms) |
| Times | Africa/Lagos (WAT, UTC+1). Every post block carries its own time. |
| Phone tool | `EASYWURLD_POST_COMMANDER.html` — one file, works offline, copy button on every block, tick marks that save on the phone. |
| Spreadsheet | `content_calendar.csv` — one row per post, dated, editable on your phone in Google Sheets. |
| Brand rules | `marketing/brand-prompt.md`. Never invent results, ratings or testimonials. |

**How to read a post block.** Each post has one core idea and up to four platform versions.
The platform versions are different writing, not the same caption pasted four times.

**The daily mix.** 7 entertain, 3 teach, 1 sell — one job per post, never two.
Memes sit near 20% of output.

**Scheduling a month ahead.** Everything below is dated. Open the sheet, sort by date, and
schedule the whole month in one sitting. The app is for the days when something needs
writing on the spot.

# Day 1 · Sunday · Sunday 1 Nov 2026 — Everything you were told

**Offer in focus:** The Clear Homepage Blueprint · CLEAR

## Post 01 · This bakery's homepage says 'welcome'

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Recognition plus relief. They have seen this page a hundred times without being able to say what was wrong with it.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Screen recording + talking head, 26s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> This bakery's whole first screen says 'Welcome to our website'.

**On-screen beats**
- Not ugly. Unclear
- A stranger learns nothing in 4 seconds
- Say what you sell, when it is ready
- Then one button

**Voiceover script**
> This bakery's whole first screen says welcome to our website. Not ugly, unclear. A stranger lands, learns nothing in four seconds, and leaves. Say the thing only you can say. Bread out of the oven at 6am, Agege. Then one button: order for pickup. That is the whole page.

**Caption**
> Not ugly. Unclear. The first screen has one job: tell a stranger what you sell and what to do next.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of a cream-toned fake bakery page, terracotta arrow on the headline.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel, 4:5

**Slide copy**
1. 'Welcome to our website'
2. Is not a headline
3. It is a doormat
4. Say what you sell + when it is ready
5. Then one button

**Caption**
> Most Lagos homepages open with a welcome. A stranger does not want to be welcomed. They want to know if you sell the thing they came for.
> 
> Write what you sell. Say when it is ready. Add one button.

**Hashtags:** #LagosSME #WebDesignNigeria #Easywurld #SmallBusinessTips

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Text post, 90 words

> Open your homepage as if you have never seen it. Can you tell what they sell in four seconds?
> 
> Most pages open with 'welcome to our website'. That is a doormat, not a headline. A stranger is standing in the doorway deciding whether to come in.
> 
> Try this: name what you sell, say when it is ready, and give one button. 'Bread out of the oven at 6am, Agege. Order for pickup.'
> 
> That is the whole first screen.
> 
> What does your first screen say right now?

## Post 02 · 'Committed to excellence'

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A shared embarrassment everyone recognises in their own copy.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Kinetic type, 14s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Every Nigerian business is 'committed to excellence'. Nobody has ever felt excellence.

**On-screen beats**
- Excellence is not a promise
- It is a Tuesday
- Say the thing you do before 9am
- That is the proof

**Voiceover script**
> Every Nigerian business is committed to excellence. Nobody has ever felt excellence. You cannot feel a commitment. You can feel that the order came in twenty minutes. Say the thing you actually do before nine a.m. and let that be the promise.

**Caption**
> Excellence is a word. 'We confirm every order within 20 minutes' is a promise you can keep.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Relatable #Easywurld #SmallBusinessTips

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 10:00 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Committed to excellence
2. Said nobody's customer, ever
3. Say what happens, and when

**Caption**
> If your About page could belong to any business in Nigeria, it is not describing your business.
> 
> Replace one adjective with one promise.

**Hashtags:** #Copywriting #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 10:30 · Text post, 80 words

> 'Committed to excellence since 2014.'
> 
> Nobody has ever felt excellence. They feel that their order was confirmed in twenty minutes, or that somebody picked up on the second ring.
> 
> A promise you can check beats an adjective you cannot. Replace one line on your About page this week with something that happens on a normal Tuesday.
> 
> What is one thing your business actually does, every single time?

## Post 03 · The three-part price reply

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Relief. It gives them the exact fix for a moment they lived through this morning.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type, 22s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your price reply ends with a question mark. That is where the sale dies.

**On-screen beats**
- Price
- What it includes, one line
- One question that moves forward
- No question mark on the price

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked how much. You said fifteen thousand, do you want it? That question hands the decision back to a person who was already deciding. Say the price, say what it includes in one line, then ask something that moves forward. Send your address and I will reserve one. One sentence. No question mark on the price.

**Caption**
> Price. What it includes. One question that moves it forward. That is the whole reply.

**Hashtags:** #WhatsAppBusiness #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld #SalesTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 12:30 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. 'How much?'
2. '₦15,000. Do you want it?'
3. That question is the problem
4. Say the price, then what happens next
5. ₦15,000. Delivered Wednesday. Send your address.

**Caption**
> The question mark at the end of a price is where the chat goes quiet.
> 
> Say the price. Say what happens next. Say nothing else.

**Hashtags:** #WhatsAppBusiness #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 12:00 · Text post, 100 words

> A customer asks how much. You reply '₦15,000, do you want it?' and the chat goes quiet.
> 
> You think they lost interest. They did not. You asked them a question they were not ready to answer, and gave them nothing to do next.
> 
> Try this instead: '₦15,000. Delivered Wednesday. Send your address and I will reserve one.'
> 
> Price. What happens next. No question mark. That one change is worth more than most ad budgets.
> 
> What is your standard price reply? Paste it and I will tell you what it is missing.

## Post 04 · Your logo is not the problem

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Relief from a worry they have been carrying.

### TikTok · 13:45 · Talking head, 18s

- Nobody can find the button

**Voiceover script**

**Caption**
> Nobody ever left a business because the logo was ordinary. They left because they could not tell what to do next.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #HotTake #Easywurld #Branding

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Instagram · 14:15 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Your logo is fine
2. Line four is not
3. 'Solutions' is not a thing you sell

**Caption**

**Hashtags:** #Branding #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Facebook · 13:30 · Text post, 85 words

> A new logo feels like progress because it is visible and it finishes in a week.
> 
> Meanwhile line four of the homepage still says 'solutions', the button is invisible on a phone, and the prices are in a PDF.
> 
> Nobody has ever left a business because the logo was ordinary. They leave because they could not work out what to do next. Fix the reading order first. The mark can wait.
> 
> If a stranger read only your homepage, what would they think you sell?

## Post 05 · Forty packs a day, cracked screen

That is the whole system. We spend months on branding and she is beating us with a cracked screen and a fast reply.

**Caption**

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #StoryTime #Easywurld

**Visual:** Talking head, slow zoom out, no music under the first 10 seconds.

### Facebook · 15:00 · Text post, 95 words

> 
> No website. No logo. No 'committed to excellence'. She answers every message in about four minutes and remembers your name by the second order.
> 
> We spend months on the things you can photograph and skip the thing you cannot: how fast somebody hears back.
> 
> If you only fix one thing this month, fix the reply.
> 
> How long does a new customer wait before they hear from you?

## Post 06 · The four-second test

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** An action they can take in the next two minutes, on their own phone.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Screen recording, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Open your website. Look away. Look back. Can you say what they sell?

**On-screen beats**
- Four seconds
- No scrolling allowed
- If you hesitate, they left
- Rewrite the first line

**Voiceover script**
> Open your website. Look away for four seconds. Look back. Can you say what they sell without scrolling? If you hesitated, a stranger left. Not because the site is bad, but because the first screen is decoration. Rewrite one line today: what you sell, who for, what to do next.

**Caption**
> Do it on your own site right now. Four seconds. No scrolling.

**Hashtags:** #WebsiteTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #SmallBusinessTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Look away for 4 seconds
2. Look back
3. Can you say what they sell?
4. No scrolling allowed
5. If you hesitated, so did they

**Caption**
> The four-second test. Run it on your own homepage tonight. It is free and it is brutal.

**Hashtags:** #WebDesign #LagosBusiness #Easywurld

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post, 90 words

> Try this on your own site tonight.
> 
> Open the homepage. Look away for four seconds. Look back. Can you say what the business sells, who it is for, and what to do next, without scrolling?
> 
> If you hesitated, so did every stranger this month.
> 
> It is not a design problem. It is a first-sentence problem, and it costs one evening to fix.
> 
> Tell me what you sell in one sentence and I will tell you if a stranger could guess it.

## Post 07 · Make it pop

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A shared joke that lands with anyone who has ever been briefed badly.

**Caption**
> 'Make it pop' has cost more money in this country than any other three words.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #Design

### Instagram · 18:30 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. 'Make it pop'
2. Is not a brief

**Caption**

**Hashtags:** #DesignHumor #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 18:00 · Text post, 75 words

> 'Just make it pop.'
> 
> Three words that have cost Nigerian businesses more money than any other feedback. Pop is not a direction, so every designer guesses, and you pay for the guesses.
> 
> Try: 'I want a stranger to press the order button.' Now it is a brief, and the job takes a day.
> 
> What is the vaguest feedback you have ever received on your business?

## Post 08 · What does a website cost?

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Reach. It is funny, local, and every business owner has an opinion.

### TikTok · 19:00 · Street interview, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> I asked twelve Lagos traders what a website costs.

**On-screen beats**
- Nine did not know
- Two said a number
- One had one and hated it
- Nobody knew what it should do

**Voiceover script**
> I asked twelve Lagos traders what a website costs. Nine said they did not know. Two gave me a number. One already had a website and told me it was useless, which turned out to mean nobody visited it. Not one of them told me what a website should do for them. That is the part nobody is selling.

**Caption**
> Nobody in the market is confused about the price. They are confused about the point.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #StreetInterview #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Handheld, market or street background, real sound, captions burned in.

### Instagram · 19:30 · Reel + caption

**Script slides**
1. 12 traders
2. 9 did not know
3. 1 had one, hated it
4. 0 knew what it was for

**Caption**
> The price question is easy. The question underneath it is 'what will change?' Answer that and the price stops being a problem.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #StreetInterview #Easywurld

**Visual:** Handheld, market or street background, real sound, captions burned in.

### Facebook · 19:30 · Text post, 100 words

> I asked twelve Lagos traders what a website costs.
> 
> Nine did not know. Two gave me a number. One already had a site and said it was useless, which turned out to mean nobody visited it.
> 
> Not one of them told me what a website should do for them. That is the gap. The industry sells pages. Business owners are trying to buy customers, and nobody is connecting the two.
> 
> If you are thinking about a site, start with the change you want, not the page count.
> 
> What would have to change for a website to be worth ₦150,000 to you?

If a stranger looked at your website and left, that is the homepage pack. If you are about to build a site and do not know what to decide first, that is the launch pack.
> 
> One pack. One problem. And to be honest about the limit: none of them will bring you customers. They give you the scripts and templates for the customers you already get.

> What was the actual problem this week? I will tell you which pack, or tell you you don't need one.

## Post 10 · The ₦180,000 we turned down

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Proof of character that cannot be faked, and the most shareable format we have.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 55s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> We turned down ₦180,000 last month.

**On-screen beats**
- A restaurant wanted a rebuild
- Their site was fine
- They answered DMs at 11pm
- We spent the money on replies instead

**Voiceover script**
> We turned down a hundred and eighty thousand naira last month. A restaurant in Lekki wanted a full rebuild. We opened their site and it was fine. Slow, ugly, but it loaded, prices were there, the WhatsApp button worked. So we asked how many people who message you actually order. One in five, they said. Their site was not the problem. Their replies were. We told them to keep the site and spend the money on answering messages before nine a.m. Their site is still ugly. Orders went up.

**Caption**
> We would rather tell you not to spend. It is better business, and it is the only marketing that cannot be faked.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #HonestBusiness #SmallBusinessNigeria

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Instagram · 20:45 · Reel + caption

**Script slides**
1. They wanted a ₦180,000 rebuild
2. Their site was fine
3. Their replies were the problem
4. We said keep the site

**Caption**
> A slow, ugly site that works beats a beautiful one that answers tomorrow. Tell us your problem and we will tell you honestly if you need us.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #HonestBusiness #Easywurld

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post, 130 words

> We turned down ₦180,000 last month.
> 
> A restaurant in Lekki wanted a full rebuild. We opened their site. Slow, ugly, but it loaded, the prices were there, and the WhatsApp button worked. So we asked the only question that mattered: how many people who message you actually order?
> 
> One in five, they said.
> 
> The site was not the problem. The reply was. We told them to keep the site and spend the money on answering messages before 9am.
> 
> Their site is still ugly. Their orders went up.
> 
> We would rather tell you not to spend. It is better business, and it is the only marketing that cannot be faked.
> 
> Have you ever paid for the wrong fix? Tell me what you bought and what the real problem turned out to be.

## Post 11 · Follow up three times

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** The single highest-value habit in the pack, given away free.

### TikTok · 21:30 · Kinetic type, 24s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Most sales in Nigeria happen after the third follow-up. Most businesses stop at one.

**On-screen beats**
- Day 1: the quote
- Day 3: one useful line
- Day 7: the close
- Set the dates before you send

**Voiceover script**
> Most sales happen after the third follow-up. Most businesses stop at one because the second feels like begging. It is not begging if you add something. Day one, the quote. Day three, one useful line, a delivery date or a photo. Day seven, a close: should I keep this for you or release it? Set those three dates before you send the quote.

**Caption**
> Day 1 the quote. Day 3 something useful. Day 7 the close. Three dates, set before you send.

**Hashtags:** #SalesTips #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld #WhatsAppBusiness

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 21:15 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. You sent the quote
2. And waited
3. Day 3: add something useful
4. Day 7: 'keep it or release it?'
5. Three follow-ups, not one

**Caption**
> The second message is not begging if it carries something new. A date, a photo, a slot that is filling.

**Hashtags:** #SalesTips #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 21:45 · Text post, 105 words

> Most businesses send a quote and then wait. If nothing comes back, they assume the customer lost interest.
> 
> Usually the customer just got busy, and the quote is now 40 messages up their chat list.
> 
> Three follow-ups: day 1 the quote, day 3 something useful (a date, a photo, a slot that is filling), day 7 the close. 'Should I keep this for you or release it?'
> 
> Set the three dates before you press send. It takes ten seconds and it is the cheapest thing in this post.
> 
> How many times do you follow up? Be honest.

## Post 01 · The WhatsApp profile that answers nothing

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Almost every viewer can check their own profile in ten seconds.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Screen recording, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> This business answers nothing before you even say hello.

**On-screen beats**
- No catalogue
- No hours
- No delivery info
- Three taps to nowhere

**Voiceover script**
> This WhatsApp Business profile answers nothing before you say hello. No catalogue, no opening hours, no delivery information. So every customer has to ask the same four questions, and the owner answers them forty times a day. Set the greeting, the away message and one catalogue item. That is an afternoon, and it buys back an hour a day.

**Caption**
> An unanswered profile is a shop with no sign. Set the greeting, the away message, one catalogue item.

**Hashtags:** #WhatsAppBusiness #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessTips

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel

3. No delivery info

> Check your WhatsApp Business profile as a stranger.
> 
> Is there a catalogue? Opening hours? Delivery cost? Payment details?
> 
> If not, every single customer has to ask you the same four questions, and you answer them forty times a day. That is an hour of your life, every day, spent typing the same thing.

## Post 03 · 'Let me think about it'

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** The exact words for the hardest moment in a sales chat.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type, 23s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> 'Let me think about it' is not an objection. It is a request for time you never gave.

**On-screen beats**
- Ask what they are weighing
- Offer a smaller next step
- Name the date
- Then leave them alone

**Voiceover script**
> Let me think about it is not an objection. It is a request for time you never offered. So offer it. Ask what they are weighing, price or timing. Then give a smaller next step: I will hold one until Friday. Name the date, then leave them alone. The silence after a clear next step is not rejection.

**Caption**
> 'Let me think about it' usually means 'you did not give me a small enough next step'.

**Hashtags:** #SalesTips #WhatsAppBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 12:30 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. 'Let me think about it'
2. Is not a no
3. It is a request for time
4. Give a smaller next step
5. 'I'll hold one until Friday'

**Caption**
> Name a date. Then stop talking. The silence after a clear next step is not rejection.

**Hashtags:** #SalesTips #LagosSME #Easywurld

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 12:00 · Text post, 105 words

> 'Let me think about it.'
> 
> Every seller hears this and treats it as a no. It is usually a request for time you never offered.
> 
> Offer it. 'Of course. Are you weighing the price or the timing?' Then give a smaller next step: 'I can hold one until Friday, then I release it.'
> 
> Name the date. Then leave them alone. If they come back, they come back to a person who was not desperate at them.
> 
> What do you normally reply when a customer says 'let me think about it'?

## Post 05 · The quote that was never followed up

**On-screen beats**
- I was going to buy
- I got busy
- He never followed up
- Someone else did

**Voiceover script**

I got busy, the message sank, and two days later I bought from someone who asked me one question on the third day. He did not lose a sale because of price. He lost it because he assumed silence meant no.

**Caption**
> He did not lose the sale on price. He lost it on day three.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #StoryTime #SalesTips #Easywurld

### Instagram · 15:45 · Reel + caption

2. He sent it
3. Then never again

**Hashtags:** #SalesTips #LagosBusiness #Easywurld

### Facebook · 15:00 · Text post, 110 words

> I asked a vendor for a price. He sent it and never spoke to me again.
> 
> I was going to buy. Then work happened, the message sank down my chat list, and two days later I bought from someone else who asked me one question on the third day: 'Still need this for the weekend?'
> 
> He did not lose that sale to price. He lost it on day three.
> 
> If you send quotes, put the follow-up date in your phone the moment you send it. Not because you are desperate. Because they are busy.
> 
> Do you follow up, or do you wait to be chased?

## Post 01 · A price for everything, a reason for nothing

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Every food vendor recognises their own menu board in three seconds.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Screen recording, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> This menu has a price for everything and a reason for nothing.

**On-screen beats**
- Jollof ₦2,500. Why?
- Add one line under each item
- What is in it, who it feeds
- Now the price has a job

**Voiceover script**
> This menu has a price for everything and a reason for nothing. Jollof, two thousand five hundred. Chicken, three thousand. Now add one line under each item: what is in it, and who it actually feeds. Rice, two pieces, one person, very hungry. Now the price has a job, and nobody has to ask you a question before they decide.

**Caption**
> A price without a reason is a question. One line under each item answers it before it is asked.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel, 4:5

**Slide copy**
1. Jollof · ₦2,500
2. Chicken · ₦3,000
3. Why?
4. Say what is in it and who it feeds
5. Now the price has a job

**Caption**
> Your menu is a sales page. Give every price a reason and the questions stop.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Text post

> Look at your menu, price list or catalogue. Every item has a number and no reason.
> 
> Jollof, ₦2,500. Chicken, ₦3,000. A customer has to ask what is in it, how big it is, and whether it feeds one person or four. You answer that question forty times a day.
> 
> Add one line under each item: what is in it and who it feeds. Rice, two pieces, one hungry person.
> 
> The price does not change. The number of questions does.
> 
> What is the item you answer the most questions about?

## Post 02 · 'How much?' is not a price question

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A reframe that turns a defensive moment into a useful one.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Kinetic type, 14s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> "How much?" is not a price question.

**On-screen beats**
- It is: is this for me?
- They cannot see the value yet
- Give the context before the number
- Then the number lands

**Voiceover script**
> How much is not a price question. It is, is this for me. They cannot see what they are paying for yet, so any number looks like a lot. Give one line of context before the price and watch the same number land differently.

**Caption**
> Nobody can judge a price without knowing what it is for.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 10:00 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. "How much?"
2. = "Is this for me?"
3. Context first, number second

**Caption**
> One line of context before your price changes how it is heard.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 10:30 · Text post

> When a customer asks 'how much?', we hear a price question. It is not.
> 
> It is 'is this for me?' They cannot see what they would be paying for, so every number looks big.
> 
> Give one line of context before the price: what it is, who it is for, what happens after. The same number lands completely differently.
> 
> What is your one line of context?

## Post 03 · Say the price once, early

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A habit change that removes the most common customer question.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type, 21s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Hidden prices do not read as premium. They read as expensive and negotiable.

**On-screen beats**
- No price = assume the worst
- They must message to find out
- Most will not
- Say it early, say it once

**Voiceover script**
> Hiding your price does not make you look premium. It makes you look expensive and negotiable at the same time. A stranger assumes the worst number and does not bother asking. Say the price early, in the first three lines. The people who cannot afford it stop wasting your evening, and the ones who can finally get to the point.

**Caption**
> Hidden price = expensive and negotiable. Both at once.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 12:30 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. No price
2. = assume the worst
3. = message to find out
4. = most won't
5. Say it in the first three lines

**Caption**
> You are not hiding the price from competitors. You are hiding it from buyers.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 12:00 · Text post

> A lot of businesses hide their prices to look premium. It does not work that way.
> 
> No price reads as two things at once: too expensive for me, and negotiable. A stranger assumes the worst and does not ask.
> 
> Say the price early and say it once. The people who cannot afford it stop wasting your evening. The ones who can get straight to the point.
> 
> Do you show prices? Why or why not?

## Post 04 · Discounting is the most expensive way to feel busy

**Caption**

### Facebook · 13:30 · Text post

> 
> Worse, they trained their customers. Now nobody buys at the normal price, because they know January is coming.
> 
> Discount dead stock. Discount a slot that would otherwise be empty. Do not discount to feel wanted.

## Post 06 · The quote is price, scope, date

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** The most copied line of the month, applied to the harder document.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 23s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> A quote with no date is a question, and they cannot answer a question.

**On-screen beats**
- Price
- What is included, in lines
- What is not included
- A date, then a yes/no

**Voiceover script**
> A quote with no date is a question, and nobody can say yes to a question. Five lines: the price, what is included, what is not included, the date it will be finished, and one sentence they can reply yes to. The what is not included line is the one that wins the job, because it is the only line that sounds honest.

**Caption**
> Price, scope, exclusions, date, one yes-shaped question.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. The price
2. What is included
3. What is not included
4. The date it lands
5. "Shall I book the slot?"

**Caption**
> The exclusions line wins more jobs than the price does.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> Most quotes are one line: a number and 'let me know'.
> 
> That is a question, and a customer cannot say yes to a question.
> 
> Five lines: the price, what is included, what is not included, when it will be finished, and one sentence they can reply to. 'Shall I book the slot?'
> 
> The line about what is not included wins more jobs than the price, because it is the only one that sounds honest.
> 
> What does your quote look like right now?

## Post 07 · Budget? Any budget.

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** The exact meeting everyone has sat through.

### TikTok · 18:00 · Two-line skit, 12s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Me: what is your budget? Client: any budget.

**On-screen beats**
- That is not a budget
- It is a hope
- Give three tiers instead
- One recommendation

**Voiceover script**
> What is your budget. Any budget. That is not a budget, that is a hope. So do not ask. Give three options at three prices and one recommendation. People who do not know what things cost can still choose between three things.

**Caption**
> Never ask for a budget. Offer three prices and one opinion.

## Post 11 · Never quote a range

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** One line that removes an entire negotiation.

### TikTok · 21:30 · Kinetic type, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Never quote a range in a first reply.

**On-screen beats**
- ₦50,000 to ₦80,000
- They heard ₦50,000
- Everything above is a discussion
- Quote once, at the top

**Voiceover script**
> Never quote a range in a first reply. Fifty to eighty thousand is heard as fifty thousand, and everything above it becomes a negotiation. If the price depends on the job, quote the smallest version and describe it. Otherwise quote one number and stand behind it.

**Caption**
> A range is a discount you have already given.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 21:15 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. "₦50,000 to ₦80,000"
2. They heard ₦50,000
3. Everything above is a fight

**Caption**
> Quote the smallest version, or quote one number. Not a range.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 21:45 · Text post

> Never quote a range in a first reply.
> 
> '₦50,000 to ₦80,000' is heard as ₦50,000. Everything above that becomes a negotiation you did not agree to have, and you usually lose it because you already said the number.
> 
> If the price really depends on the job, quote the smallest version and describe what that gets them. Otherwise quote one number and stand behind it.
> 
> Have you ever quoted a range and regretted it?

## Post 01 · Nine seconds to load

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Almost everyone can check this on their own site tonight.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Screen recording with a timer, 24s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> This page takes nine seconds to load on mobile data.

**On-screen beats**
- Nine seconds is a bus ride
- Two 4MB photos do that
- Compress before upload
- Back under three

**Voiceover script**
> This page takes nine seconds to load on mobile data. Nine seconds is long enough to open another tab, get distracted, and buy from someone else. The cause is almost always two images that were never compressed. Run them through a compressor before uploading and this page is under three seconds.

**Caption**
> Nine seconds is not slow. Nine seconds is a customer leaving.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 5-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Nine seconds on mobile data
2. Two uncompressed photos
3. That is the whole cause
4. Compress before upload
5. Under three seconds

**Caption**
> Most slow sites are one enormous photo wearing a nice dress.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Text post

> Open your site on mobile data, not wifi, and count the seconds.
> 
> Nine seconds is long enough for a customer to open another tab and buy from someone else.
> 
> The cause is almost always one or two photos straight from a phone camera, four megabytes each, uploaded at full size. Compress them before uploading and most pages drop under three seconds.
> 
> It is a ten minute fix that has been costing you for a year.
> 
> How fast is yours?

## Post 03 · The thumb test

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A two-minute test they will actually run.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type, 22s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Hold your phone in one hand and try to buy from your own site.

**On-screen beats**
- One hand only
- Can you reach the button?
- Can you read without zooming?
- Fix what you cannot reach

**Voiceover script**
> The thumb test. Hold your phone in one hand and try to buy from your own website. Can you reach the main button without moving your hand? Can you read the first line without zooming? Everything you cannot reach with a thumb is a customer who left. Move the button up and make the text bigger. That is the whole fix.

**Caption**
> One hand. One thumb. Everything else is decoration.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

> Hold your phone in one hand. Open your own website. Now try to buy something without moving your hand or using a second finger.

## Post 04 · An entrance animation is a tax

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A position that will annoy designers and delight business owners.

### TikTok · 13:45 · Talking head, 18s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> An animation that delays your price is a tax on your customer.

**On-screen beats**
- Two seconds of nothing
- On data
- To reveal a number
- Motion should reveal, not delay

**Voiceover script**
> An animation that delays your price is a tax on your customer. Two seconds of nothing, on mobile data, so that a heading can slide in. Motion is fine when it reveals something. When it delays the one thing they came for, it is decoration you are charging them for.

**Caption**
> If the animation is between the customer and the price, it is a cost, not a design.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HotTake

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

2. Fade up
3. Two seconds

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HotTake

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Facebook · 13:30 · Text post

> Hot take: entrance animations on a small business website are a tax on the customer.
> 
> Two seconds of nothing, on mobile data, so a heading can slide into place. Then the price appears.
> 
> Motion is good when it reveals something. When it stands between a customer and the one thing they came for, you are charging them for decoration.

## Post 06 · Compress before you upload

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** The single highest-return technical fix in the whole month.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Screen recording, 21s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> You do not need a developer to make your site faster. You need to stop uploading 4MB photos.

**On-screen beats**
- One photo = one second
- Resize to 1600px
- Compress to under 200kb
- Do it before upload

**Voiceover script**
> You do not need a developer to make your site faster. You need to stop uploading four megabyte photos. Resize to sixteen hundred pixels wide, compress to under two hundred kilobytes, and do it before you upload. Ten photos will take you twenty minutes and your page will load twice as fast.

**Caption**
> Resize, compress, then upload. In that order, every time.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. 4MB photo
2. = one extra second
3. Resize · compress · upload

**Caption**
> Ten photos, twenty minutes, twice the speed. No developer needed.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> You do not need a developer to make your site load faster. You need to stop uploading 4MB photos straight from your camera.
> 
> Resize to about 1600 pixels wide. Compress to under 200KB. Then upload.
> 
> Ten photos is twenty minutes of work and it will roughly halve your load time.
> 
> Most 'slow website' problems in Nigeria are three photos nobody resized.
> 
> What is the biggest image on your site right now?

## Post 07 · The caption bar is eating your button

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A five-second fix that most sites have never done.

### TikTok · 18:00 · Screen recording, 12s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> The caption bar is sitting on top of your only button.

**On-screen beats**
- Bottom 60 pixels are dead
- Your button lives there
- Move it up
- Done

**Voiceover script**
> The caption bar is sitting on top of your only button. On most phones, the bottom sixty pixels are covered by a bar or a set of buttons. If your order button lives there, nobody can press it. Move it up and test again.

**Caption**
> The bottom 60 pixels of a phone belong to the platform, not to you.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 18:30 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Bottom 60px
2. Covered
3. Put your button higher

**Caption**
> Check where your button lands on an actual phone, not in the preview.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 18:00 · Text post

> Small thing that costs businesses real money: the bottom 60-80 pixels of a phone screen.
> 
> That strip belongs to the browser or the platform. If your main button sits there, a stranger physically cannot press it, and you will never know because it looks fine in the preview.
> 
> Open your site on a real phone. Look at where the button lands. Move it up.
> 
> Five seconds of work.

## Post 10 · Fast on 2G, in three weeks

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A story about scope, told with the number that ends the conversation.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 45s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Someone asked us to make a site load in two seconds on 2G, in three weeks.

**On-screen beats**
- We said no to the 2G part
- We said yes to two seconds on 3G
- That is honest scope
- They took it

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked us to make their site load in two seconds on 2G, in three weeks. We said no to the 2G part. Two seconds on a good 3G connection is achievable and we can measure it. On 2G, nobody loads anything in two seconds, and we are not going to promise a number we cannot test. They took the version we could prove.

**Caption**
> Say the number you can measure. Refuse the one you cannot.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Instagram · 20:45 · Reel + caption

**Script slides**
1. 2 seconds, on 2G
2. No
3. 2 seconds, on 3G
4. Yes, and we can prove it

**Caption**
> A promise you cannot measure is not a standard. It is a delay before the argument.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post

> Someone asked us to make their site load in two seconds on 2G, in three weeks.
> 
> We said no to the 2G part. Two seconds on a decent 3G connection is achievable and measurable. On 2G, nobody loads anything in two seconds, and promising it just delays the argument to delivery day.
> 
> They took the version we could prove.
> 
> The next time someone promises you a number, ask them how they will measure it.
> 
> Have you ever been promised a result nobody could measure?

## Post 11 · Test on a real phone, on data

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** One line that invalidates every comfortable test they have done.

### TikTok · 21:30 · Kinetic type, 19s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Wifi lies to you every single time.

**On-screen beats**
- Your office wifi is fast
- Your preview is cached
- Their phone is on data
- Test like they do

**Voiceover script**
> Wifi lies to you. Your office connection is faster than any customer's, and your browser has already saved the images. So the page you test is not the page they get. Turn off wifi, use mobile data, open in a private window, and count again. That is the real number.

**Caption**
> Turn off wifi. Private window. Mobile data. Then look.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 21:45 · Text post

> Two things lie to you when you test your own website: your wifi and your browser cache.
> 
> Your office connection is faster than any customer's. Your browser has already saved the images, so the page you see is not the page they get.
> 
> Turn off wifi. Use mobile data. Open a private window. Now count the seconds.
> 
> That is the real number, and it is usually about twice what you thought.
> 
> What did yours turn out to be?

## Post 02 · Seen is the most expensive word

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** One word does the emotional work of the whole lesson.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Kinetic type, 14s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> "Seen" is the most expensive word in Nigerian business.

**On-screen beats**
- It is not a read receipt
- It is a decision
- They think you don't want them
- Reply, even to say later

**Voiceover script**
> Seen is the most expensive word in Nigerian business. To you it means I will get to it. To them it means this business does not want my money. Even a broken reply beats a read receipt, because a read receipt is a decision you did not mean to make.

**Caption**
> A blue tick with no reply is a no, whether you meant it or not.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 10:00 · 3-slide carousel

2. Is not a read receipt
3. It is a decision

**Caption**

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 10:30 · Text post

> 'Seen' is the most expensive word in Nigerian business.
> 
> To you it means 'I will get to it tonight'. To the customer it means 'this person does not want my money'.
> 
> A short reply that says when you will answer beats a blue tick every time. 'I can confirm by 6pm' costs four seconds and keeps the customer.
> 
> Do you reply even when you have no answer yet?

## Post 03 · The follow-up that is not begging

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** Removes the shame that stops people from following up at all.

### TikTok · 11:30 · Kinetic type, 22s

- A photo
- A slot that is filling

**Voiceover script**

**Caption**
> Begging asks. Following up gives. Add one useful thing.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

### Instagram · 12:30 · 5-slide carousel

2. = begging
3. Add a date
4. Add a photo
5. = a service

> If the message carries no new information, do not send it.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 12:00 · Text post

> Most people do not follow up because the second message feels like begging.
> 
> It feels that way because it usually only asks: 'just checking in'.
> 
> Add something. A delivery date. A photo of the finished thing. A note that you have two slots left this week, if that is actually true.
> 
> Once the message carries information, it stops being begging and starts being a service.

## Post 04 · Ghosting is a system failure

**Caption**

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Facebook · 13:30 · Text post

> We call it ghosting, as though the customer did something to us.

> 
> Fix the gap, not the customer.

## Post 07 · 'Ok' is not a yes

### Facebook · 18:00 · Text post

> 
> That is not a yes. It is not a no. It is nothing yet, and most businesses leave it exactly there and count it as a sale that fell through.
> 
> Send one line with a next step in it: 'Should I send the payment details?' Now you find out in four seconds instead of four days.

## Post 10 · No to the blast, yes to eleven messages

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Shows the alternative to automation without moralising.

**Voiceover script**
> A client asked us to automate his follow-ups. We said no, and it was not a principle, it was arithmetic. A broadcast to two thousand people would save him an hour and look exactly like what it was. So we wrote eleven personal messages to the eleven real enquiries and he sent them by hand. Forty minutes. Four replies the same day.

**Caption**

## Post 10 · The site with no prices at all

- We built one page with prices first

Three weeks in, the page with prices had four times the enquiries. He did not change his mind about how it looked. The customers did.

**Caption**
> He did not want prices on his site. So we tested it on one page instead of arguing.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

2. We built one page with them
3. Three weeks later
4. That page had 4x the enquiries

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post

> A client wanted his new website to have no prices anywhere, because he was sure that prices made a business look cheap.
> 
> Instead of arguing, we built one extra page with prices on it and left the rest of the site exactly as he wanted.
>

>

## Post 01 · The worst page I saw this week

> 
> Three fixes: move it above the fold, make it the darkest thing on the page, and say what happens when you press it.

**Caption**
> Likes measure taste. Saves measure usefulness. Only one of them pays.

## Post 02 · Twice is not harassment

## Post 03 · The day-three message, word for word

## Post 06 · Release the stock, out loud

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A real deadline is more persuasive than any adjective.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 21s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> You do not need fake scarcity. You need a real deadline.

**On-screen beats**
- "I'll release it Friday"
- True if you would
- Then release it
- The truth is the pressure

**Voiceover script**
> You do not need to invent scarcity. You need a real deadline. I will release this slot on Friday and give it to the next person. That is true, because the slot is worth money and you cannot hold it forever. The moment you say it, and mean it, the customer has a reason to decide this week without you pretending there is a queue.

**Caption**
> A real deadline does the work that fake scarcity pretends to do.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Fake: "only 2 left"
2. Real: "I'll release it Friday"
3. Then actually release it

**Caption**
> Invent a deadline once and every future deadline stops working.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> You do not need to invent scarcity. You need a real deadline.
> 
> 'I will release this slot on Friday and offer it to the next person.'
> 
> That is true, because the slot is worth money and you cannot hold it indefinitely. Say it once, then actually do it on Friday.
> 
> The difference between that and 'only 2 left' is that one of them survives being checked.
> 
> Do you have a real deadline you could name today?

## Post 10 · Automating the first message

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A principle stated as a line you can actually hold.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 40s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Someone asked us to set up auto-replies on every channel. We said no.

**On-screen beats**
- Auto-reply is a promise
- A bot cannot keep a specific promise
- So it reads as a wall
- One typed line, then automate

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked us to put auto-replies on every channel. We said no, for one reason. An automatic reply is a promise, and a promise you cannot make specific reads as a wall. The first message in a sale has to be typed by a person who can say something only true for that customer. Automate the tenth message, not the first.

**Caption**
> The first reply is the one place a human is worth more than software.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post

> Then when someone messages at 11pm, they get information instead of silence, and you wake up to a conversation that has already started properly.
> 
> Four lines. About ninety seconds of typing. It answers while you sleep.
> 
> What would your four lines say?

## Post 10 · Hide the delivery cost

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A principle with an obvious cost attached.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 32s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Someone asked us to hide the delivery cost until checkout. We said no.

**On-screen beats**
- It is legal
- It is also expensive
- Surprise fees cost more than the fee
- Show it early

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked us to keep the delivery cost out of sight until checkout. We said no. It is legal and it works, briefly, and then it costs more than the fee itself, because the customer feels caught rather than served. Show delivery early, even when it is ugly. The ones who stay are the ones who can pay, which is the only kind you want.

**Caption**
> A surprise fee costs more than the fee. Every time.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Instagram · 20:45 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Hide delivery till checkout
2. We said no
3. The surprise costs more than the fee

**Caption**
> Filters out the buyers who cannot pay, which is a kindness to both sides.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post

> Someone asked us to keep the delivery cost out of sight until checkout.
> 
> We said no. It is legal, and it works briefly, and then it costs more than the fee itself — because the customer feels caught rather than served. They abandon, and they tell people why.
> 
> Show delivery early, even when it is ugly. The ones who stay are the ones who can actually pay, which is the only kind of customer worth chasing.
> 
> Would you rather lose them early or late?

## Post 02 · A quote with no date is a question

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** The sharpest reframe in the day.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Kinetic type, 14s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> A quote with no date is not a quote. It is a question.

**On-screen beats**
- They cannot say yes
- They cannot plan
- So they ask more questions
- Give them a date

**Voiceover script**
> A quote with no date is not a quote, it is a question, because the customer cannot answer it. They do not know whether yes means next week or December, so they ask more questions, and every question is another chance for the sale to die. Give them a date and they can say yes.

**Caption**
> A date turns a number into a decision.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

### Facebook · 10:30 · Text post

> 
> The customer cannot answer it, because they do not know whether yes means next week or December. So they ask more questions, and every question is another chance for the sale to quietly die.

## Post 06 · Never a PDF first

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A small, specific, instantly obeyed rule.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 21s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Never send a PDF as your first contact with a customer.

**On-screen beats**
- They will not open it
- Or they open it at work
- Put the price in the message
- PDF after interest

**Voiceover script**
> Never send a PDF as your first contact. They will not open it, or they will open it in a queue and close it. Put the number in the message itself, one screen, no scrolling. Send the PDF after they have asked a second question, because then it is information instead of homework.

**Caption**
> A PDF is homework. A message is a decision.

## Post 10 · A job we could not deliver

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Losing work honestly is the most persuasive proof of standards.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 35s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> We were asked to quote a job we could not deliver in three weeks. We said six weeks and lost it.

**On-screen beats**
- They wanted three
- We could not
- So we told the truth
- And lost the job

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked us to quote a job for three weeks. We could not do it in three weeks without rushing something that would show. So we quoted six. They went with someone who said three, and I hope that person could actually do it. We lost the job and kept the standard, and I would do it again, which is easy to say and was not easy at the time.

**Caption**
> We quoted the truth, lost the job, and would do it again.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

3. Said: 6
4. Lost the job

> The fastest quote is not the same as the honest one.

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

> We could not do it in three weeks without rushing something that would show. So we quoted six.

## Post 11 · End with a yes-shaped question

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** One small habit with an outsized effect on replies.

### TikTok · 21:30 · Kinetic type, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> End every quote with one question: "Shall I book the slot?"

**On-screen beats**
- Yes-shaped
- One word to answer
- Has a date in it
- Then stop typing

**Voiceover script**
> End every quote with one question: shall I book the slot. It is yes shaped, it takes one word to answer, and it implies there is a slot with value. Then stop typing. No second paragraph, no let me know, no thank you so much for your time. One question, and then silence, which is where the answer comes from.

**Caption**
> One question. Then stop typing.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 21:15 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. End with a question
2. Yes-shaped
3. Then stop typing

**Caption**
> "Shall I book the slot?" is the most answerable sentence in a quote.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 21:45 · Text post

> End every quote with one question: 'Shall I book the slot?'
> 
> It is yes-shaped. It takes one word to answer. It implies the slot has value and a date.
> 
> Then stop typing. No second paragraph. No 'let me know'. No thank you for your time.
> 
> One question, then silence — which is where the answer actually comes from.
> 
> Try it on your next quote and tell me what happens.

## Post 03 · Ask what happens after launch

- Who fixes the form

Most cheap quotes stop at delivery, which is why they are cheap, and then you discover the cost in the third month when you need one line changed.

## Post 06 · Three questions for any web quote

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A takeaway they can use on someone else's pitch today.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 23s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Three questions to ask any web quote. Who owns the domain, who fixes it, who pays for hosting.

**On-screen beats**
- The domain is your name
- Not theirs
- Who fixes it in six months
- Get it in writing

**Voiceover script**
> Three questions to ask any web quote. Who owns the domain, in whose name, in writing. Who fixes it when it breaks in six months, and how much do they charge. And who pays for hosting every year. If any of those answers is vague, walk away now, because all three become emergencies at the worst possible time.

**Caption**
> Domain, fixes, hosting. Ask all three before you pay anyone.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 4-slide carousel

2. In whose name?
3. Who fixes it in 6 months?
4. Who pays for hosting?

**Caption**
> If you cannot answer all three, you do not own your website.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> Three questions to ask any website quote:
> 
> 1. Who owns the domain, in whose name, in writing?
> 2. Who fixes it when it breaks in six months, and at what cost?

## Post 03 · The first line has three jobs

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** One rule with three options, easy to apply today.

- Who it is for
- What to do next

**Voiceover script**
> Your first line has three jobs and it can only do one. Say what you sell, say who it is for, or say what to do next. Most homepages try all three at once and end up saying nothing. Pick the strongest one for your customer and write it in ten words or fewer, then leave it alone for a week.

**Caption**

## Post 04 · Design is reading order

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Reframes design from taste to function, which suits a non-designer owner.

### TikTok · 13:45 · Talking head, 20s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Design is not decoration. Design is reading order.

**On-screen beats**
- What do they see first
- Second
- Where does the eye go
- Then the button

**Voiceover script**
> Design is not decoration. Design is reading order. What do they see first, what do they see second, and where does the eye go when they get bored. If the answer is anywhere but the button, the design is decorative and it is costing you. You do not need taste for this, you need to squint at your own page.

**Caption**
> Squint at your homepage. Whatever you see first is what you are selling.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HotTake

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Instagram · 14:15 · 4-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. First
2. Second
3. Third
4. Button: last?

**Caption**
> Squint at your own page. What survives the blur is the message.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HotTake

**Visual:** Talking head to camera, one jump cut on the disagreement.

### Facebook · 13:30 · Text post

> Design is not decoration. Design is reading order.
> 
> What does the customer see first? Second? Where does their eye go when they get bored?
> 
> If the answer is anywhere other than the thing you want them to do, the design is decorative and it is costing you money.
> 
> You do not need taste to check this. Squint at your own page. Whatever survives the blur is what you are actually selling.
> 
> What do you see first?

## Post 06 · Put the proof next to the claim

**Voiceover script**

A claim on its own is a slogan. A claim with evidence attached is a fact, and facts are the only things that move money.

## Post 10 · Auto-playing sound

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A refusal everyone has suffered from as a visitor.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 30s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> A client wanted sound to play automatically. We said no.

**On-screen beats**
- It is not a mute button
- It is a close button
- Especially on a bus
- They agreed

**Voiceover script**
> A client wanted music to play the moment the site loaded. We said no. On a phone, in public, with the volume up, that is not a feature, it is an exit. They argued for a day, we built it without sound, and the enquiries went up. The fastest way to lose someone on mobile is to make a noise they did not ask for.

**Caption**
> On a phone with the volume up, sound is not a feature. It is an exit.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

2. On a phone, in public

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

## Post 01 · Seven CTAs in forty words

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Everyone has written this caption, so the shame lands as recognition.

### TikTok · 07:00 · Screen recording, 22s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> This post has seven calls to action in forty words. That means it has none.

**On-screen beats**
- Link in bio
- DM me
- Comment
- Call
- Book
- Save
- Share
- Nobody does anything

**Voiceover script**
> This post has seven calls to action in forty words. Link in bio, DM me, comment below, call this number, book a slot, save it, share it. When you ask for seven things, the reader does nothing, because choosing is work. One post, one ask. Everything else is noise you paid attention to write.

**Caption**
> Seven asks is not ambition. It is indecision, published.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Instagram · 07:30 · 4-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Link in bio
2. DM me
3. Comment · call · book
4. Seven asks = no ask

**Caption**
> Ask for one thing. The other six are why nobody did anything.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

**Visual:** Screen recording of the page, cream frame, one terracotta arrow on the problem.

### Facebook · 08:00 · Text post

> A caption I saw this week had seven calls to action in forty words: link in bio, DM me, comment below, call, book, save, share.
> 
> Seven asks is not ambition. It is indecision, published.
> 
> When you ask for seven things, the reader does nothing, because choosing is work.
> 
> One post, one ask. Write the other six down for another day.
> 
> What is the one thing you want people to do?

## Post 02 · One post, one job

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** The simplest rule in the whole month, stated flatly.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Kinetic type, 13s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> One post, one job: entertain, teach, or sell. Pick one.

**On-screen beats**
- Not two
- Not three
- One
- Then post it

**Voiceover script**
> One post, one job. Entertain, teach, or sell. Pick one and commit to it, because the post that tries to do two does neither well. The one that entertains and then asks for money loses the entertainment. The one that teaches and then sells loses the teaching. Pick one job and let the next post do the other.

**Caption**
> A post that does two jobs does neither.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 10:00 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Entertain
2. Teach
3. Sell
4. Pick one

**Caption**
> Three jobs, eleven posts a day, one job each.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Facebook · 10:30 · Text post

> One post, one job.
> 
> Entertain. Teach. Or sell.
> 
> Pick one and commit, because a post that tries to do two does neither well. The one that entertains and then asks for money loses the entertainment. The one that teaches and then sells loses the teaching.
> 
> Your next post can do the other job.
> 
> Which of the three do you default to?

## Post 03 · The one-word CTA

## Post 04 · "Only 2 left" with 40 left

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Names a practice the audience has caught others doing.

### TikTok · 13:45 · Talking head, 21s

> "Only two left" when there are forty left is the end of your credibility.

**On-screen beats**
- People check
- They always check
- Now nothing you say is true
- Including the price

**Voiceover script**
> Only two left, when there are forty left, is the end of your credibility. People check. They always check, because they have been lied to before. And once one number on your page is invented, every other number becomes a guess, including the price, which was true. Fake scarcity does not create urgency, it creates suspicion.

**Caption**

## Post 06 · Sell the limit

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** The most transferable idea of the week, usable outside our packs.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 23s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Sell the limit, not the dream. "It will not get you customers" sells better than a promise.

**On-screen beats**
- Every ad promises
- Promises sound like ads
- A limit sounds like a person
- Then they trust the rest

**Voiceover script**
> Sell the limit, not the dream. It will not get you customers, it will stop you losing the ones you have. That sentence sells better than any promise, because every ad promises and almost none admit a boundary. A limit sounds like a person telling the truth, and once they trust the limit, they trust the price.

**Caption**
> A limit sounds like a person. A promise sounds like an ad.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

2. Limits sound like people

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> 'It will not get you customers — it will stop you losing the ones you already have.'

## Post 07 · The caption for people who already want it

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Splits the audience in a way people have never considered.

### TikTok · 18:00 · Kinetic type, 17s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Your caption explains the offer to the people who already want it.

**On-screen beats**
- They do not need the paragraph
- They need the link
- The paragraph is for the others
- So write both, in order

**Voiceover script**
> Your caption explains the offer to the people who already want it. They do not need explaining. They need the link, in the first three lines, so they can act before their attention drifts. The explanation is for the ones who are not sure yet, and it can come after. Two groups, one order: price, then paragraph.

**Caption**
> Price first. Paragraph second. Two groups, one order.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

**Visual:** Flat print-style card, one large serif line, one terracotta accent.

### Instagram · 18:30 · 3-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. Already want it: need the link
2. Not sure: need the why
3. Price first, both served

**Caption**
> Stop explaining to people who already decided.

## Post 10 · Promising results in the ad

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Explains why our copy reads the way it does, which builds trust in all of it.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 31s

- Promises convert
- Then they refund

**Voiceover script**
> Someone asked us to write a promise into an ad. We said no. Promises convert, then they refund, then they resent you, and in a small market the resentment is more expensive than the sale. So we wrote the limit instead: this will not get you customers, it will stop you losing the ones you have. Fewer clicks. Better customers.

**Caption**
> Promises convert once and cost you twice.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

**Visual:** Talking head, plain cream wall, no cuts except on the turn.

### Facebook · 21:00 · Text post

> Someone asked us to write a promise into an ad.
> 
> We said no. Promises convert, then they refund, then they resent you — and in a small market the resentment is more expensive than the sale ever was.

## Post 02 · Two weeks in, the honest number

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A public ledger is the most defensible content an account can post.

### TikTok · 09:15 · Talking head, 25s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Two weeks in. Here is the number, read off the sheet, including the boring parts.

**On-screen beats**
- Views are not the point
- Conversations are
- Most posts did little
- The few did most

**Voiceover script**
> Two weeks in, and here is the number read off the sheet. Most posts did very little. A few did most of the work. The conversations that came from it were fewer than the views by a factor of about a thousand, which is normal and is the part nobody posts about. That is the honest state of it, and I would rather show you the flat line than sell you a hockey stick.

**Caption**
> Two weeks, read off the sheet. Mostly a flat line, a few spikes, and a thousand views per conversation.

**Hashtags:** #Lagos #Relatable #Easywurld #LagosBusiness

## Post 04 · Half of content strategy is answering people

- Instead it says quality and excellence
- Four paragraphs
- The real headline was free
- One line wins

**Voiceover script**
> This bakery has four paragraphs about quality and tradition, and the actual reason anyone goes there is bread out of the oven at six in the morning, in Agege. That is a headline. It tells you what it is, where it is, and why it is different, in eleven words. The paragraph cost them money. The line would have made them some.

**Caption**
> Bread out of the oven at 6am, Agege. That is the whole headline.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #WebsiteDesign #SmallBusinessNigeria #Easywurld

> Your best sentence is the one you think is too obvious to write down.

## Post 03 · Rewrite a competitor in three lines

- What they sell
- Who for
- What to do
- That is the whole exercise

**Voiceover script**
> Find a competitor's homepage and rewrite it in three lines. What they sell, who it is for, what to do next. It takes four minutes because you do not care about being clever. Then do yours, and notice how much harder it is, because now your ego is involved. The exercise is easier on someone else for exactly that reason.

**Caption**
> Three lines: what, who, next. Do a competitor first. Yours is harder.

## Post 06 · The one-sentence description

**Type:** Teach · **Why it works:** A fill-in-the-blank tool is the most shareable format there is.

### TikTok · 16:30 · Kinetic type, 24s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> One sentence: we do this, for who, so they can do that.

**On-screen beats**
- Fill it in
- Say it out loud
- If it is long
- It is two sentences

**Voiceover script**
> The sentence is: we do this, for these people, so they can do that. We build one page websites for Lagos small businesses so they can stop explaining themselves in every chat. Fill it in for your business, say it out loud, and if it takes more than one breath, you have written two sentences and you need to pick the better one.

**Caption**
> We [do this] for [who] so they can [result]. One breath or it is two sentences.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Instagram · 17:15 · 4-slide carousel

**Slide copy**
1. We [do this]
2. for [who]
3. so they can [result]
4. One breath

**Caption**
> Screenshot this and fill it in with a pen. Not a keyboard.

**Hashtags:** #SmallBusinessTips #LagosSME #Easywurld #MarketingTips

**Visual:** Kinetic type on cream, one line at a time, mark bottom-right.

### Facebook · 16:30 · Text post

> The one-sentence description:
> 
> 'We [do this] for [who] so they can [result].'
> 
> Example: 'We build one-page websites for Lagos small businesses so they can stop explaining themselves in every chat.'
> 
> Fill it in. Say it out loud. If it takes more than one breath, you have written two sentences and you need to pick the better one.
> 
> What is yours? Put it in the comments.

## Post 10 · Told a business to stop advertising

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Advice against our own interest is the strongest trust signal available.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 32s

**On-screen beats**
- They were spending
- Getting messages
- Losing them at hello
- So the spend was making the leak worse

**Voiceover script**
> We told a business to stop advertising for a month. They were spending every week, getting messages, and losing almost all of them in the first reply, because the first reply took two days. More reach made it worse, because more people arrived to be ignored. We fixed the reply, then they started advertising again, and it worked, which is why I am telling you to stop.

**Caption**
> Advertising into a slow reply is paying to be ignored at scale.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

> Eleven minutes produced six customers who did not previously exist, and nobody had to be persuaded of anything.

> Is your pin right?

## Post 10 · Ranking for "Lagos food"

- Then the next

## Post 05 · Asked at the counter, not by email

Reviews are not about asking nicely, they are about asking at the exact moment gratitude is highest, which is about forty seconds after delivery.

> Someone offered to get us reviews for a small fee. We said no.
> 
> Bought reviews are the only mistake in this business you cannot undo. One fake can be reported. The pattern of them can be spotted. And when it is, every real review you have becomes suspect too.
> 
> You would be trading six years of trust for eleven numbers.
> 
> Have you ever suspected a shop of this?

## Post 04 · Copywriting is removal

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** Reframes the task from writing to cutting, which is easier.

### TikTok · 13:45 · Talking head, 18s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> Copywriting is not cleverness. It is removal.

**On-screen beats**
- First draft: three sentences
- Best line: one
- Delete the first two
- That is the edit

**Voiceover script**
> Copywriting is not cleverness, it is removal. The first draft is three sentences. The best line is one of them, usually the last. So delete the first two and you are done, and it will be better than the version you were proud of, which is annoying and reliable in equal measure.

**Caption**
> Your best line was the last sentence. Delete the two before it.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HotTake

## Post 06 · Write like you talk at the counter

## Post 10 · The word "disruptive"

**Type:** Entertain · **Why it works:** A story about testing a word instead of arguing about it.

### TikTok · 20:45 · Talking head, 32s

**Hook (0-3s)**
> A client insisted on the word disruptive. We kept it. Nothing happened. Then we removed it.

**On-screen beats**
- He loved it
- It meant nothing to his customers
- We tested it
- The plain version won

**Voiceover script**
> A client wanted the word disruptive in his headline. We argued, then we stopped arguing and tested it: two versions, same ad, one week. The disruptive version got fewer clicks and two comments asking what it meant. We removed it and put back the plain sentence, which was about delivery times. He still says disruptive in meetings. The page does not.

**Caption**
> We tested the word instead of arguing about it. The plain version won.

**Hashtags:** #LagosBusiness #Easywurld #SmallBusinessNigeria #HonestBusiness

## Post 03 · Lead with one offer for thirty days

Not because the others are bad, but because thirty days is the shortest honest test of whether an offer can carry attention.
